# More verbs A-D

> Further verbs A-D — what a coverage check against two large verb lists showed was still missing.

[← The grammar of verbs](../README.md) &middot; [All groups](README.md)

Gathered by checking the catalog against roughly 21,000 scraped verb lemmas and writing entries for what was genuinely absent.

Most of that list was not worth writing — archaic verbs no living American uses, British-only spellings, narrow technical coinages, and a fair number of words that were not verbs at all — so these are the survivors of a deliberate rejection pass that threw out roughly nine of every ten candidates.

---
### abdicate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈæbdɪkeɪt/ &middot; *AB-di-kayt*
**Русский:** отрекаться от престола, отречься; слагать с себя (обязанности)
**Forms:** *abdicate* &middot; *abdicated* &middot; *abdicated*
**Third person:** *abdicates* &middot; **-ing form:** *abdicating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*he abdicated the throne*), intransitive (*the king abdicated in 1936*)

To give up a throne, a position of power, or a responsibility that belongs to you.

1. A monarch may *abdicate* in favor of a younger heir.
2. Edward VIII *abdicated* in order to marry an American.
3. Critics say the agency has *abdicated* its duty to protect consumers.

---

### abet

**Pronunciation:** /əˈbɛt/ &middot; *uh-BET*
**Русский:** подстрекать, пособничать; содействовать (преступлению)
**Forms:** *abet* &middot; *abetted* &middot; *abetted*
**Third person:** *abets* &middot; **-ing form:** *abetting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The final *t* doubles: *abetted, abetting*. In American legal English it almost always appears in the pair *aid and abet*.

To help or encourage someone to do something wrong, especially a crime.

1. Anyone who helps *abet* a fraud can be charged too.
2. He *abetted* the robbery by driving the getaway car.
3. She was convicted of aiding and *abetting* the escape.

---

### abhor

**Pronunciation:** /əbˈhɔːr/ &middot; *uhb-HOR*
**Русский:** питать отвращение, ненавидеть; возненавидеть
**Forms:** *abhor* &middot; *abhorred* &middot; *abhorred*
**Third person:** *abhors* &middot; **-ing form:** *abhorring*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The final *r* doubles: *abhorred, abhorring*. Like *hate*, it is a state verb, so the continuous (*I am abhorring*) sounds wrong.

To hate something very strongly, usually because you think it is morally wrong.

1. I *abhor* cruelty of any kind.
2. She *abhorred* the idea of asking her family for money.
3. He has always *abhorred* violence, even in self-defense.

---

### abound

**Pronunciation:** /əˈbaʊnd/ &middot; *uh-BOWND*
**Русский:** изобиловать, иметься в изобилии
**Forms:** *abound* &middot; *abounded* &middot; *abounded*
**Third person:** *abounds* &middot; **-ing form:** *abounding*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** To name what is plentiful, use *abound with* or *abound in*: *the lake abounds with fish*.

To exist in very large numbers or quantities.

1. Rumors *abound* about who will get the job.
2. Wildflowers *abounded* on the hillside that spring.
3. Theories have *abounded* ever since the story broke.

---

### abrade

**Pronunciation:** /əˈbreɪd/ &middot; *uh-BRAYD*
**Русский:** стирать, сдирать (поверхность); истереть
**Forms:** *abrade* &middot; *abraded* &middot; *abraded*
**Third person:** *abrades* &middot; **-ing form:** *abrading*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The related words are *abrasion* (the injury) and *abrasive* (the adjective).

To wear away or scrape a surface by rubbing against it.

1. Wind and sand slowly *abrade* the sandstone.
2. The rope *abraded* his palms before he let go.
3. The paint had been *abraded* by years of weather.

---

### abrogate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈæbrəɡeɪt/ &middot; *AB-ruh-gayt*
**Русский:** отменять, аннулировать (закон, договор); упразднить
**Forms:** *abrogate* &middot; *abrogated* &middot; *abrogated*
**Third person:** *abrogates* &middot; **-ing form:** *abrogating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To cancel a law, treaty, or agreement by official action.

1. No president can *abrogate* a treaty on his own, the senators argued.
2. The new government *abrogated* the agreement within a month.
3. Several clauses have quietly been *abrogated* since then.

---

### abscond

**Pronunciation:** /əbˈskɑːnd/ &middot; *uhb-SKAHND*
**Русский:** скрываться, сбегать (с деньгами); скрыться от правосудия
**Forms:** *abscond* &middot; *absconded* &middot; *absconded*
**Third person:** *absconds* &middot; **-ing form:** *absconding*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** You *abscond with* money and *abscond from* a place.

To leave quickly and secretly, usually after stealing something or to avoid arrest.

1. If the suspects *abscond*, the bank loses the money for good.
2. The treasurer *absconded* with nearly a million dollars.
3. Two defendants have *absconded* while out on bail.

---

### abstain

**Pronunciation:** /əbˈsteɪn/ &middot; *uhb-STAYN*
**Русский:** воздерживаться, воздержаться
**Forms:** *abstain* &middot; *abstained* &middot; *abstained*
**Third person:** *abstains* &middot; **-ing form:** *abstaining*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive — always with *from*, or absolutely in the voting sense
**Contrast:** Takes *from*, never a bare object: ✗ *abstain alcohol* → *abstain from alcohol*. In a vote it stands alone — *three members abstained* — and means neither yes nor no, which is not the same as not voting at all.

To hold back deliberately from something you could have done or taken.

1. Two senators *abstained* rather than vote either way.
2. She *abstains* from caffeine after noon.
3. He has *abstained* from alcohol for six years.

---

### abut

**Pronunciation:** /əˈbʌt/ &middot; *uh-BUT*
**Русский:** примыкать, граничить (вплотную)
**Forms:** *abut* &middot; *abutted* &middot; *abutted*
**Third person:** *abuts* &middot; **-ing form:** *abutting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*their yard abuts the highway*), intransitive (*their yard abuts on the highway*)
**Contrast:** The final *t* doubles: *abutted, abutting*.

To touch something along one side, or to share a border with it.

1. Their property *abuts* the national forest.
2. The new wing *abutted* the old library wall.
3. Houses *abutting* the freeway get the worst of the noise.

---

### accent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈæksɛnt/ &middot; *AK-sent*
**Русский:** подчёркивать, выделять; акцентировать
**Forms:** *accent* &middot; *accented* &middot; *accented*
**Third person:** *accents* &middot; **-ing form:** *accenting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** *Accent* is the everyday word for emphasizing a detail; *accentuate* is its more formal cousin.

To make one part of something stand out, or to stress a syllable or word.

1. Designers often *accent* a plain room with one bright color.
2. She *accented* the wrong syllable and everyone smiled.
3. The dish is *accented* with lemon and fresh mint.

---

### acclimatize

**Pronunciation:** /əˈklaɪmətaɪz/ &middot; *uh-KLY-muh-tyz*
**Русский:** акклиматизироваться, привыкать к климату; акклиматизировать
**Forms:** *acclimatize* &middot; *acclimatized* &middot; *acclimatized*
**Third person:** *acclimatizes* &middot; **-ing form:** *acclimatizing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — intransitive (*climbers acclimatize slowly*), transitive (*they acclimatized the plants first*)
**Contrast:** Americans more often say the shorter *acclimate*; British English spells this verb *acclimatise*.

To get used to a new climate, altitude, or set of conditions, or to help someone do so.

1. Climbers need a week to *acclimatize* before the summit push.
2. We *acclimatized* to the heat after a few days.
3. The seedlings are still *acclimatizing* to life outdoors.

---

### accord

**Pronunciation:** /əˈkɔːrd/ &middot; *uh-KORD*
**Русский:** предоставлять, оказывать (права, почести); согласовываться
**Forms:** *accord* &middot; *accorded* &middot; *accorded*
**Third person:** *accords* &middot; **-ing form:** *according*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*the law accords them equal rights*), intransitive (*his story accords with the video*)
**Contrast:** In the intransitive sense it takes *with*. The fixed phrases are *in accordance with* and *of one's own accord*.

To formally give someone a right, an honor, or a kind of treatment, or to agree with something.

1. The statute *accords* refugees the same protections as citizens.
2. The city *accorded* the astronaut a hero's welcome.
3. His account does not *accord* with what the cameras recorded.

---

### accost

**Pronunciation:** /əˈkɔːst/ &middot; *uh-KAWST*
**Русский:** приставать (к прохожему), подходить с разговором; окликнуть
**Forms:** *accost* &middot; *accosted* &middot; *accosted*
**Third person:** *accosts* &middot; **-ing form:** *accosting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To go up to a stranger and speak to them in a bold or threatening way.

1. Panhandlers sometimes *accost* tourists outside the station.
2. A reporter *accosted* him as he left the courthouse.
3. She was *accosted* by two men in the parking garage.

---

### accuse

**Pronunciation:** /əˈkjuːz/ &middot; *uh-KYOOZ*
**Русский:** обвинять, обвинить
**Forms:** *accuse* &middot; *accused* &middot; *accused*
**Third person:** *accuses* &middot; **-ing form:** *accusing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive — *accuse someone **of** something*
**Contrast:** The preposition is **of**, and it takes a noun or *-ing*, never an infinitive: ✗ *accused him to steal* → *accused him of stealing*. Compare *charge someone **with*** and *blame someone **for***, which take different prepositions for much the same idea. *The accused* as a noun means the defendant.

To say that someone has done something wrong or illegal.

1. They *accused* him of leaking the report.
2. She was *accused* of cheating and cleared a week later.
3. I'm not *accusing* anyone — I'm asking.

---

### accustom

**Pronunciation:** /əˈkʌstəm/ &middot; *uh-KUS-tuhm*
**Русский:** приучать, приучить (к чему-либо)
**Forms:** *accustom* &middot; *accustomed* &middot; *accustomed*
**Third person:** *accustoms* &middot; **-ing form:** *accustoming*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** It takes *to* plus a noun or an *-ing* form: *accustomed to waiting*, never "accustomed to wait".

To make yourself or someone else get used to something.

1. It takes a few minutes to *accustom* your eyes to the dark.
2. He *accustomed* himself to getting up at five.
3. We are not *accustomed* to this kind of cold.

---

### adjoin

**Pronunciation:** /əˈdʒɔɪn/ &middot; *uh-JOYN*
**Русский:** примыкать, прилегать, граничить
**Forms:** *adjoin* &middot; *adjoined* &middot; *adjoined*
**Third person:** *adjoins* &middot; **-ing form:** *adjoining*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*the kitchen adjoins the porch*), intransitive (*the two lots adjoin*)
**Contrast:** Do not mix up *adjoin* (to be next to) with *adjourn* (to end a meeting): *the rooms adjoin*, but *the court adjourns*.

To be next to something and touching it.

1. The two offices *adjoin* each other.
2. A small garden *adjoined* the kitchen.
3. They booked *adjoining* rooms at the hotel.

---

### adjudicate

**Pronunciation:** /əˈdʒuːdɪkeɪt/ &middot; *uh-JOO-di-kayt*
**Русский:** выносить решение, рассматривать (дело); рассудить
**Forms:** *adjudicate* &middot; *adjudicated* &middot; *adjudicated*
**Third person:** *adjudicates* &middot; **-ing form:** *adjudicating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*a judge will adjudicate the claim*), intransitive (*the panel adjudicates on appeals*)

To make an official decision about a dispute or a claim.

1. A federal judge will *adjudicate* the dispute next month.
2. The board *adjudicated* more than two hundred claims last year.
3. The case is still being *adjudicated*.

---

### adulterate

**Pronunciation:** /əˈdʌltəreɪt/ &middot; *uh-DUL-tuh-rayt*
**Русский:** разбавлять, фальсифицировать (продукт); подмешивать примеси
**Forms:** *adulterate* &middot; *adulterated* &middot; *adulterated*
**Third person:** *adulterates* &middot; **-ing form:** *adulterating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** *Adulterate* is about mixing something impure into a product; *commit adultery* is about an unfaithful marriage. The two are not interchangeable.

To make a food, drink, or drug impure or weaker by adding something cheaper to it.

1. Some sellers *adulterate* honey with cheap corn syrup.
2. Inspectors found that the milk had been *adulterated* with water.
3. The company was fined for *adulterating* its olive oil.

---

### aerate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛreɪt/ &middot; *ER-ayt*
**Русский:** насыщать воздухом, аэрировать; проветривать (почву)
**Forms:** *aerate* &middot; *aerated* &middot; *aerated*
**Third person:** *aerates* &middot; **-ing form:** *aerating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To let air into something such as soil, water, or wine.

1. You should *aerate* the lawn in early fall.
2. He *aerated* the wine by pouring it into a decanter.
3. The tank is *aerated* by a small electric pump.

---

### affect

**Pronunciation:** /əˈfɛkt/ &middot; *uh-FEKT*
**Русский:** влиять, воздействовать; повлиять, затронуть
**Forms:** *affect* &middot; *affected* &middot; *affected*
**Third person:** *affects* &middot; **-ing form:** *affecting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** *Affect* is the verb, *effect* the noun: *the storm affected flights*, but *the storm had an effect on flights*. (*Effect* as a verb exists — *effect change* — but it is formal and rare.)

To have an effect on someone or something, or to move someone emotionally.

1. Rising interest rates *affect* everyone with a mortgage.
2. The storm *affected* flights all along the coast.
3. She was deeply *affected* by the news of his death.

---

### affiliate

**Pronunciation:** /əˈfɪlieɪt/ &middot; *uh-FIL-ee-ayt*
**Русский:** присоединяться, вступать в объединение; быть связанным (с организацией)
**Forms:** *affiliate* &middot; *affiliated* &middot; *affiliated*
**Third person:** *affiliates* &middot; **-ing form:** *affiliating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — intransitive (*the clinic affiliated with a hospital*), transitive (*they affiliated the school with the university*)
**Contrast:** The verb ends in *-ayt* (uh-FIL-ee-ayt); the noun and adjective end in *-ut* (uh-FIL-ee-ut): *a local affiliate*.

To join or connect formally with a larger organization.

1. Many small clinics *affiliate* with a teaching hospital.
2. The union *affiliated* with a national federation in 2019.
3. She is *affiliated* with three research centers.

---

### affix

**Pronunciation:** /əˈfɪks/ &middot; *uh-FIKS*
**Русский:** прикреплять, наклеивать; поставить (подпись, печать)
**Forms:** *affix* &middot; *affixed* &middot; *affixed*
**Third person:** *affixes* &middot; **-ing form:** *affixing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The verb is stressed on the second syllable (uh-FIKS); the grammar noun *affix*, meaning a prefix or suffix, is AF-iks. Third person adds *-es*: *affixes*.

To attach or stick something firmly onto a surface.

1. Please *affix* a stamp to the upper right corner.
2. He *affixed* his signature to the contract.
3. A warning label had been *affixed* to the box.

---

### afflict

**Pronunciation:** /əˈflɪkt/ &middot; *uh-FLIKT*
**Русский:** поражать (о болезни), причинять страдания; мучить
**Forms:** *afflict* &middot; *afflicted* &middot; *afflicted*
**Third person:** *afflicts* &middot; **-ing form:** *afflicting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** A problem *afflicts* a person, but a person *inflicts* a problem on someone: *arthritis afflicted her*; *he inflicted pain on them*.

To cause someone or something to suffer, especially from an illness or a lasting problem.

1. Droughts *afflict* the region almost every summer.
2. A rare disease *afflicted* both brothers.
3. The city is *afflicted* by a chronic housing shortage.

---

### aggrandize

**Pronunciation:** /əˈɡrændaɪz/ &middot; *uh-GRAN-dyz*
**Русский:** возвеличивать, превозносить (себя); возвысить
**Forms:** *aggrandize* &middot; *aggrandized* &middot; *aggrandized*
**Third person:** *aggrandizes* &middot; **-ing form:** *aggrandizing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The common form is the compound *self-aggrandizing*; British English spells the verb *aggrandise*.

To make someone or something look greater or more important than they really are.

1. Politicians rarely miss a chance to *aggrandize* themselves.
2. He *aggrandized* his part in the rescue with every retelling.
3. The legend has been *aggrandized* over the years.

---

### aggregate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈæɡrɪɡeɪt/ &middot; *AG-ri-gayt*
**Русский:** объединять, собирать воедино; агрегировать (данные)
**Forms:** *aggregate* &middot; *aggregated* &middot; *aggregated*
**Third person:** *aggregates* &middot; **-ing form:** *aggregating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The verb ends in *-gayt* (AG-ri-gayt); the noun and adjective end in *-guht* (AG-ri-guht), as in *in the aggregate*.

To gather separate items or figures together into a single group or total.

1. The site *aggregates* headlines from a hundred newspapers.
2. We *aggregated* three years of sales data.
3. Scores are *aggregated* at the end of the season.

---

### aggrieve

**Pronunciation:** /əˈɡriːv/ &middot; *uh-GREEV*
**Русский:** ущемлять, обижать; ущемить (права)
**Forms:** *aggrieve* &middot; *aggrieved* &middot; *aggrieved*
**Third person:** *aggrieves* &middot; **-ing form:** *aggrieving*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** You will meet it mostly as the participle *aggrieved*, which acts like an adjective: *the aggrieved employees*.

To treat someone unfairly, so that they have a real reason to complain.

1. A ruling like this is bound to *aggrieve* somebody.
2. He felt deeply *aggrieved* at being passed over for the job.
3. The *aggrieved* party has thirty days to appeal.

---

### agitate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈædʒɪteɪt/ &middot; *AJ-i-tayt*
**Русский:** агитировать, добиваться (перемен); волновать, взволновать
**Forms:** *agitate* &middot; *agitated* &middot; *agitated*
**Third person:** *agitates* &middot; **-ing form:** *agitating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — intransitive (*students agitated for lower fees*), transitive (*the noise agitated the dogs*)
**Contrast:** In the campaigning sense it takes *for* or *against*: *agitate for reform*, *agitate against the tax*.

To campaign noisily for a change, or to make someone upset and restless.

1. Workers began to *agitate* for shorter hours.
2. The long wait *agitated* the patients.
3. He gets *agitated* whenever the subject comes up.

---

### agonize

**Pronunciation:** /ˈæɡənaɪz/ &middot; *AG-uh-nyz*
**Русский:** мучиться, терзаться (сомнениями); ломать голову
**Forms:** *agonize* &middot; *agonized* &middot; *agonized*
**Third person:** *agonizes* &middot; **-ing form:** *agonizing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** It takes *over* or *about*. British English spells it *agonise*.

To worry about a decision for a long time in a painful, drawn-out way.

1. Don't *agonize* over it — just pick one.
2. She *agonized* for weeks before saying no.
3. He has been *agonizing* about whether to sell the house.

---

### ail

**Pronunciation:** /eɪl/ &middot; *AYL*
**Русский:** недомогать, хворать; беспокоить, мучить
**Forms:** *ail* &middot; *ailed* &middot; *ailed*
**Third person:** *ails* &middot; **-ing form:** *ailing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*what ails you?*), intransitive (*his ailing mother*)
**Contrast:** *Ail* sounds exactly like *ale*, the drink. The living form is the participle *ailing*: *an ailing economy*.

To trouble someone or make them unwell, or to be in poor health.

1. Tell me what *ails* you.
2. No one could say what *ailed* the old engine.
3. He spent the year caring for his *ailing* mother.

---

### air

**Pronunciation:** /ɛr/ &middot; *AIR*
**Русский:** транслировать, выходить в эфир; высказывать (претензии, мнение)
**Forms:** *air* &middot; *aired* &middot; *aired*
**Third person:** *airs* &middot; **-ing form:** *airing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*the network airs it on Thursdays*), intransitive (*the finale airs tonight*)
**Contrast:** *Air out* (a room) is a separate phrasal verb about fresh air; plain *air* is about broadcasting or speaking out.

To broadcast a program, or to state an opinion or complaint publicly.

1. The new season *airs* Thursday at eight.
2. The network *aired* the interview twice.
3. Both sides have *aired* their grievances in public.

---

### airbrush

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛrbrʌʃ/ &middot; *AIR-brush*
**Русский:** ретушировать (аэрографом), приукрашивать; заретушировать
**Forms:** *airbrush* &middot; *airbrushed* &middot; *airbrushed*
**Third person:** *airbrushes* &middot; **-ing form:** *airbrushing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Because it ends in *-sh*, the third person adds *-es*: *airbrushes*.

To retouch a picture with an airbrush, or to remove awkward details from a record.

1. Magazines still *airbrush* their cover photos.
2. The scar had been *airbrushed* out of the portrait.
3. He accused the museum of *airbrushing* him out of its own history.

---

### airdrop

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛrdrɑːp/ &middot; *AIR-drahp*
**Русский:** сбрасывать с самолёта (груз, десант); сбросить на парашютах
**Forms:** *airdrop* &middot; *airdropped* &middot; *airdropped*
**Third person:** *airdrops* &middot; **-ing form:** *airdropping*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The final *p* doubles: *airdropped, airdropping*.

To drop supplies or troops from an aircraft, usually by parachute.

1. Planes will *airdrop* food into the cut-off valley.
2. The army *airdropped* medical supplies overnight.
3. Aid is being *airdropped* to villages the flood has isolated.

---

### airlift

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɛrlɪft/ &middot; *AIR-lift*
**Русский:** перебрасывать по воздуху, эвакуировать вертолётом
**Forms:** *airlift* &middot; *airlifted* &middot; *airlifted*
**Third person:** *airlifts* &middot; **-ing form:** *airlifting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To carry people or goods by aircraft, especially in an emergency.

1. Helicopters *airlift* injured climbers off the mountain every summer.
2. Rescuers *airlifted* the driver to a trauma center.
3. Two hundred tons of supplies have been *airlifted* in.

---

### allay

**Pronunciation:** /əˈleɪ/ &middot; *uh-LAY*
**Русский:** рассеивать (страхи), успокаивать; унять, развеять
**Forms:** *allay* &middot; *allayed* &middot; *allayed*
**Third person:** *allays* &middot; **-ing form:** *allaying*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The *y* stays because a vowel comes before it: *allayed*, not "allaid". Its usual objects are *fears, concerns, doubts, suspicions*.

To make someone's fear, doubt, or anger less strong.

1. The mayor tried to *allay* fears about the water supply.
2. His calm answers *allayed* most of the criticism.
3. Nothing the company has said has *allayed* investors' doubts.

---

### allude

**Pronunciation:** /əˈluːd/ &middot; *uh-LOOD*
**Русский:** намекать, намекнуть; ссылаться
**Forms:** *allude* &middot; *alluded* &middot; *alluded*
**Third person:** *alludes* &middot; **-ing form:** *alluding*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive — always with *to*
**Contrast:** Needs *to* and cannot take an object: ✗ *he alluded the problem* → *alluded to the problem*. It means to hint, not to mention — if the thing is named outright, the verb is *refer to* or *mention*. Do not confuse it with *elude* (to escape) or *delude* (to deceive), which sound close and mean nothing like it.

To refer to something indirectly, without naming it outright.

1. She *alluded* to the argument but never described it.
2. He keeps *alluding* to some earlier version of the plan.
3. The report has *alluded* to cost overruns twice already.

---

### amalgamate

**Pronunciation:** /əˈmælɡəmeɪt/ &middot; *uh-MAL-guh-mayt*
**Русский:** объединять, сливать (компании); объединяться, слиться
**Forms:** *amalgamate* &middot; *amalgamated* &middot; *amalgamated*
**Third person:** *amalgamates* &middot; **-ing form:** *amalgamating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*they amalgamated the three offices*), intransitive (*the two banks amalgamated*)

To combine two or more organizations or things into a single one.

1. The plan is to *amalgamate* the three departments.
2. Two regional banks *amalgamated* last spring.
3. The company was formed by *amalgamating* four small carriers.

---

### amble

**Pronunciation:** /ˈæmbəl/ &middot; *AM-buhl*
**Русский:** брести, прогуливаться неспешно
**Forms:** *amble* &middot; *ambled* &middot; *ambled*
**Third person:** *ambles* &middot; **-ing form:** *ambling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** The silent *-e* drops before *-ing*: *ambling*.

To walk at a slow, relaxed pace.

1. On Sundays they *amble* through the park for an hour.
2. He *ambled* over to the counter and ordered coffee.
3. We spent the morning *ambling* along the waterfront.

---

### ameliorate

**Pronunciation:** /əˈmiːljəreɪt/ &middot; *uh-MEEL-yuh-rayt*
**Русский:** улучшать, смягчать (положение); облегчить
**Forms:** *ameliorate* &middot; *ameliorated* &middot; *ameliorated*
**Third person:** *ameliorates* &middot; **-ing form:** *ameliorating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To make a bad or painful situation better.

1. Cash payments can *ameliorate* the worst effects of a recession.
2. The new rules *ameliorated* conditions in the camps.
3. Nothing has *ameliorated* the housing shortage so far.

---

### amortize

**Pronunciation:** /ˈæmərtaɪz/ &middot; *AM-ur-tyz*
**Русский:** погашать (долг) частями, амортизировать; списывать стоимость
**Forms:** *amortize* &middot; *amortized* &middot; *amortized*
**Third person:** *amortizes* &middot; **-ing form:** *amortizing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** British English spells it *amortise*.

To pay off a debt in regular installments, or to spread the cost of an asset over several years.

1. Most buyers *amortize* the loan over thirty years.
2. The company *amortized* the software over five years.
3. The equipment is being *amortized* at ten percent a year.

---

### amputate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈæmpjuteɪt/ &middot; *AM-pyoo-tayt*
**Русский:** ампутировать, отнимать (конечность)
**Forms:** *amputate* &middot; *amputated* &middot; *amputated*
**Third person:** *amputates* &middot; **-ing form:** *amputating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To cut off a limb in a surgical operation.

1. Doctors may have to *amputate* the foot.
2. Surgeons *amputated* his leg below the knee.
3. She has learned to ski since her arm was *amputated*.

---

### anesthetize

**Pronunciation:** /əˈnɛsθətaɪz/ &middot; *uh-NES-thuh-tyz*
**Русский:** обезболивать, давать наркоз; анестезировать
**Forms:** *anesthetize* &middot; *anesthetized* &middot; *anesthetized*
**Third person:** *anesthetizes* &middot; **-ing form:** *anesthetizing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American English writes *anesthetize* and *anesthesia*; British English writes *anaesthetise* and *anaesthesia*.

To give someone a drug so that they feel no pain, usually before surgery.

1. They will *anesthetize* the whole area before stitching it.
2. The vet *anesthetized* the dog for the X-ray.
3. The patient was fully *anesthetized* within a minute.

---

### angle

**Pronunciation:** /ˈæŋɡəl/ &middot; *ANG-guhl*
**Русский:** наклонять, поворачивать под углом; наклонить
**Forms:** *angle* &middot; *angled* &middot; *angled*
**Third person:** *angles* &middot; **-ing form:** *angling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*angle the lamp toward the wall*), intransitive (*the trail angles left*)
**Contrast:** The silent *-e* drops before *-ing*: *angling*. The phrasal *angle for* (a compliment, an invitation) means to hint for something instead.

To turn or set something at a slant.

1. *Angle* the camera down a little.
2. She *angled* her chair away from the door.
3. He stood there *angling* the antenna until the picture cleared.

---

### annex

**Pronunciation:** /əˈnɛks/ &middot; *uh-NEKS*
**Русский:** аннексировать, присоединять (территорию)
**Forms:** *annex* &middot; *annexed* &middot; *annexed*
**Third person:** *annexes* &middot; **-ing form:** *annexing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The verb is stressed on the second syllable (uh-NEKS); the noun *annex*, a building added onto another, is AN-eks. Third person adds *-es*: *annexes*.

To take control of a territory and add it to your own state or city.

1. Few governments accept any right to *annex* a neighbor's land.
2. Russia *annexed* Crimea in 2014.
3. The village was *annexed* by the city in the 1950s.

---

### annihilate

**Pronunciation:** /əˈnaɪəleɪt/ &middot; *uh-NY-uh-layt*
**Русский:** уничтожать, истреблять; уничтожить, разгромить
**Forms:** *annihilate* &middot; *annihilated* &middot; *annihilated*
**Third person:** *annihilates* &middot; **-ing form:** *annihilating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The *h* is silent: uh-NY-uh-layt.

To destroy something completely, or to beat an opponent crushingly.

1. One bad quarter could *annihilate* the company's savings.
2. The storm *annihilated* the entire fishing fleet.
3. They were *annihilated* 45–0 in the opener.

---

### anoint

**Pronunciation:** /əˈnɔɪnt/ &middot; *uh-NOYNT*
**Русский:** помазать (на царство), назначать преемником; умащать елеем
**Forms:** *anoint* &middot; *anointed* &middot; *anointed*
**Third person:** *anoints* &middot; **-ing form:** *anointing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** One *n* in the middle: *anoint*, not "annoint".

To put oil on someone in a religious ceremony, or to publicly choose someone as a successor.

1. Party leaders were quick to *anoint* him as the next candidate.
2. The archbishop *anointed* the queen with oil.
3. She has been *anointed* the heir to the family business.

---

### ape

**Pronunciation:** /eɪp/ &middot; *AYP*
**Русский:** обезьянничать, подражать; копировать (манеру)
**Forms:** *ape* &middot; *aped* &middot; *aped*
**Third person:** *apes* &middot; **-ing form:** *aping*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The silent *-e* drops before *-ing*: *aping*.

To copy someone's style or behavior, usually clumsily or without understanding it.

1. Cheap brands *ape* the packaging of the originals.
2. The film *aped* Hitchcock down to the camera angles.
3. He got laughs by *aping* the principal's walk.

---

### appall

**Pronunciation:** /əˈpɔːl/ &middot; *uh-PAWL*
**Русский:** ужасать, приводить в ужас; потрясти, шокировать
**Forms:** *appall* &middot; *appalled* &middot; *appalled*
**Third person:** *appalls* &middot; **-ing form:** *appalling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American English writes *appall / appalls*; British English writes *appal / appals*. Both spell the other forms *appalled* and *appalling*.

To shock someone deeply and fill them with dismay.

1. Conditions in the shelter *appall* every visitor.
2. It *appalled* me that nobody called the police.
3. We were *appalled* by the cost of the repairs.

---

### append

**Pronunciation:** /əˈpɛnd/ &middot; *uh-PEND*
**Русский:** добавлять (в конце), прилагать; приписать
**Forms:** *append* &middot; *appended* &middot; *appended*
**Third person:** *appends* &middot; **-ing form:** *appending*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** You *append* something *to* a document.

To add something at the end of a document, a list, or a file.

1. You can *append* your comments to the report.
2. She *appended* a short note to the email.
3. A list of sources has been *appended* to the study.

---

### apportion

**Pronunciation:** /əˈpɔːrʃən/ &middot; *uh-POR-shuhn*
**Русский:** распределять, разделять (доли); распределить
**Forms:** *apportion* &middot; *apportioned* &middot; *apportioned*
**Third person:** *apportions* &middot; **-ing form:** *apportioning*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To divide something up and give out the shares.

1. The court will *apportion* the costs between the two sides.
2. They *apportioned* the profits equally among the partners.
3. Seats in the House are *apportioned* by population.

---

### apprise

**Pronunciation:** /əˈpraɪz/ &middot; *uh-PRYZ*
**Русский:** уведомлять, ставить в известность; известить
**Forms:** *apprise* &middot; *apprised* &middot; *apprised*
**Third person:** *apprises* &middot; **-ing form:** *apprising*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** *Apprise* (uh-PRYZ) means to inform; *appraise* (uh-PRAYZ) means to judge the value of something. You *apprise* someone *of* the news, but you *appraise* a house.

To formally inform someone of something.

1. Please *apprise* me of any change in the schedule.
2. The lawyer *apprised* her client of his rights.
3. Staff were *apprised* of the decision by email.

---

### appropriate

**Pronunciation:** /əˈproʊprieɪt/ &middot; *uh-PROH-pree-ayt*
**Русский:** присваивать, заимствовать; выделять (средства)
**Forms:** *appropriate* &middot; *appropriated* &middot; *appropriated*
**Third person:** *appropriates* &middot; **-ing form:** *appropriating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Stress falls the same way, but the ending changes: the verb is uh-PROH-pree-**ayt**, while the adjective meaning "suitable" is uh-PROH-pree-**ut**.

To take something for your own use without permission, or to set money aside officially for a purpose.

1. Congress must *appropriate* the funds before the agency can spend them.
2. The label *appropriated* the design without credit or payment.
3. Critics say the brand has *appropriated* a sacred symbol.

---

### approve

**Pronunciation:** /əˈpruːv/ &middot; *uh-PROOV*
**Русский:** одобрять, утверждать; одобрить, утвердить
**Forms:** *approve* &middot; *approved* &middot; *approved*
**Third person:** *approves* &middot; **-ing form:** *approving*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*the board approved the budget*), intransitive (*her parents did not approve*)
**Contrast:** Without a preposition it means to give official permission (*approve the plan*); with *of* it means to think well of something (*approve of the plan*).

To officially accept a plan or request, or to think well of something.

1. The council will *approve* the budget on Tuesday.
2. Regulators *approved* the drug last year.
3. The loan has already been *approved* by the bank.

---

### arc

**Pronunciation:** /ɑːrk/ &middot; *AHRK*
**Русский:** описывать дугу, лететь по дуге; изогнуться дугой
**Forms:** *arc* &middot; *arced* &middot; *arced*
**Third person:** *arcs* &middot; **-ing form:** *arcing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** Unlike *panic → panicked*, *arc* adds no *k* in standard American spelling: *arced, arcing*.

To move or curve along the path of an arc.

1. Fireworks *arc* over the harbor every Fourth of July.
2. The ball *arced* toward the top corner of the net.
3. Lightning was *arcing* between the two towers.

---

### arch

**Pronunciation:** /ɑːrtʃ/ &middot; *AHRCH*
**Русский:** выгибать, изгибать дугой; выгибаться, выгнуться
**Forms:** *arch* &middot; *arched* &middot; *arched*
**Third person:** *arches* &middot; **-ing form:** *arching*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*she arched her back*), intransitive (*the bridge arches over the river*)
**Contrast:** Because it ends in *-ch*, the third person adds *-es*: *arches*.

To bend something into a curve, or to form a curve over something.

1. Cats *arch* their backs when they are startled.
2. She *arched* an eyebrow but said nothing.
3. Tall elms go *arching* over the whole avenue.

---

### armor

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɑːrmər/ &middot; *AHR-mur*
**Русский:** покрывать бронёй, бронировать
**Forms:** *armor* &middot; *armored* &middot; *armored*
**Third person:** *armors* &middot; **-ing form:** *armoring*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American English spells it *armor, armored, armoring*; British English writes *armour, armoured, armouring*.

To cover something with a protective layer of metal or other hard material.

1. The company *armors* trucks for use in war zones.
2. They *armored* the embassy doors after the attack.
3. Every vehicle in the convoy has been *armored*.

---

### arouse

**Pronunciation:** /əˈraʊz/ &middot; *uh-ROWZ*
**Русский:** вызывать, пробуждать (интерес, подозрение)
**Forms:** *arouse* &middot; *aroused* &middot; *aroused*
**Third person:** *arouses* &middot; **-ing form:** *arousing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Do not confuse *arouse*, which is regular (*aroused, aroused*), with *arise*, which is irregular (*arose, arisen*).

To stir up a feeling or reaction in someone.

1. Questions like that *arouse* suspicion immediately.
2. His sudden wealth *aroused* the interest of the tax office.
3. The story has *aroused* strong feelings on both sides.

---

### arraign

**Pronunciation:** /əˈreɪn/ &middot; *uh-RAYN*
**Русский:** предъявлять обвинение в суде, привлекать к суду
**Forms:** *arraign* &middot; *arraigned* &middot; *arraigned*
**Third person:** *arraigns* &middot; **-ing form:** *arraigning*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The *g* is silent: *arraign* is uh-RAYN. You are *arraigned* **on** a charge.

To bring someone before a court to hear the charges and answer them.

1. Prosecutors will *arraign* the suspect on Monday morning.
2. She was *arraigned* on three counts of fraud.
3. The court has *arraigned* all four defendants.

---

### ascertain

**Pronunciation:** /ˌæsərˈteɪn/ &middot; *as-ur-TAYN*
**Русский:** выяснять, выяснить, устанавливать
**Forms:** *ascertain* &middot; *ascertained* &middot; *ascertained*
**Third person:** *ascertains* &middot; **-ing form:** *ascertaining*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To find something out for certain by checking the facts.

1. We need to *ascertain* who was in the building that night.
2. Investigators *ascertained* the cause of the fire within a week.
3. The police have not yet *ascertained* how the thieves got in.

---

### ascribe

**Pronunciation:** /əˈskraɪb/ &middot; *uh-SKRYB*
**Русский:** приписывать, приписать
**Forms:** *ascribe* &middot; *ascribed* &middot; *ascribed*
**Third person:** *ascribes* &middot; **-ing form:** *ascribing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive — *ascribe something **to** someone or something*
**Contrast:** The preposition is **to**, and the order is thing-first: *ascribe the delay to the weather*, never ✗ *ascribe the weather the delay*. *Attribute* is the commoner everyday synonym and behaves identically. The near-twin *subscribe to* means to agree with, which is a different verb entirely.

To say that something was caused by, or belongs to, a particular person or thing.

1. Historians *ascribe* the collapse to a single bad harvest.
2. The painting was long *ascribed* to a student of Rembrandt.
3. She is *ascribing* motives he never had.

---

### asphyxiate

**Pronunciation:** /æsˈfɪksieɪt/ &middot; *as-FIK-see-ayt*
**Русский:** удушать, задохнуться
**Forms:** *asphyxiate* &middot; *asphyxiated* &middot; *asphyxiated*
**Third person:** *asphyxiates* &middot; **-ing form:** *asphyxiating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (the fumes *asphyxiated* two workers), intransitive (the miners *asphyxiated* underground)

To kill or be killed by a lack of oxygen.

1. Carbon monoxide can *asphyxiate* a person in minutes.
2. Two workers were *asphyxiated* by fumes inside the tank.
3. The gas was slowly *asphyxiating* everyone in the basement.

---

### aspire

**Pronunciation:** /əˈspaɪər/ &middot; *uh-SPY-ur*
**Русский:** стремиться, мечтать (о чём-л.)
**Forms:** *aspire* &middot; *aspired* &middot; *aspired*
**Third person:** *aspires* &middot; **-ing form:** *aspiring*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** *aspire* takes **to**: *aspire to a position*, *aspire to be something*. Never "aspire for".

To have a strong desire to reach something important.

1. Many of these students *aspire* to a career in medicine.
2. She *aspired* to be the first in her family to finish college.
3. He has always *aspired* to something bigger than this town.

---

### assail

**Pronunciation:** /əˈseɪl/ &middot; *uh-SAYL*
**Русский:** атаковать, обрушиваться (с критикой)
**Forms:** *assail* &middot; *assailed* &middot; *assailed*
**Third person:** *assails* &middot; **-ing form:** *assailing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To attack someone or something violently, with blows or with words.

1. Critics *assail* the mayor every time the budget comes up.
2. Reporters *assailed* the senator with questions about the leak.
3. The coast has been *assailed* by three storms this month.

---

### assert

**Pronunciation:** /əˈsɜːrt/ &middot; *uh-SURT*
**Русский:** утверждать, заявлять; отстаивать
**Forms:** *assert* &middot; *asserted* &middot; *asserted*
**Third person:** *asserts* &middot; **-ing form:** *asserting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To state something firmly as true, or to claim a right with confidence.

1. The report *asserts* that the numbers were changed on purpose.
2. She *asserted* her right to speak to a lawyer.
3. He has *asserted* his innocence from the very first day.

---

### assimilate

**Pronunciation:** /əˈsɪməleɪt/ &middot; *uh-SIM-uh-layt*
**Русский:** усваивать, усвоить; ассимилироваться
**Forms:** *assimilate* &middot; *assimilated* &middot; *assimilated*
**Third person:** *assimilates* &middot; **-ing form:** *assimilating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*assimilate* new information), intransitive (the family *assimilated* quickly)

To take something in and absorb it fully, or to become part of a larger group.

1. It takes weeks to *assimilate* this much new material.
2. Her grandparents *assimilated* within a few years of arriving.
3. The team is still *assimilating* the data from last season.

---

### astound

**Pronunciation:** /əˈstaʊnd/ &middot; *uh-STOWND*
**Русский:** изумлять, поражать, поразить
**Forms:** *astound* &middot; *astounded* &middot; *astounded*
**Third person:** *astounds* &middot; **-ing form:** *astounding*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To shock someone with surprise or amazement.

1. The size of the crowd will *astound* you.
2. His answer *astounded* the whole committee.
3. We were *astounded* by how quickly the work was finished.

---

### atone

**Pronunciation:** /əˈtoʊn/ &middot; *uh-TOHN*
**Русский:** искупать, искупить (вину)
**Forms:** *atone* &middot; *atoned* &middot; *atoned*
**Third person:** *atones* &middot; **-ing form:** *atoning*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** *atone* takes **for**: *atone for a mistake*, never "atone a mistake".

To make up for something wrong that you have done.

1. Nothing he does now can *atone* for that decision.
2. She *atoned* for the mistake by working through the weekend.
3. He has spent years *atoning* for the harm he caused.

---

### atrophy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈætrəfi/ &middot; *AT-ruh-fee*
**Русский:** атрофироваться, слабеть, угасать
**Forms:** *atrophy* &middot; *atrophied* &middot; *atrophied*
**Third person:** *atrophies* &middot; **-ing form:** *atrophying*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (weeks in bed *atrophy* the muscles), intransitive (the muscles *atrophy*)
**Contrast:** The *-y* becomes *-ie-*: *atrophies, atrophied*, but the *-ing* form keeps it: *atrophying*.

To waste away and grow weak from lack of use.

1. Muscles *atrophy* quickly when a leg is in a cast.
2. His Russian *atrophied* after ten years away from home.
3. The program has *atrophied* since the funding was cut.

---

### attune

**Pronunciation:** /əˈtuːn/ &middot; *uh-TOON*
**Русский:** настраивать, приучать (к чему-л.)
**Forms:** *attune* &middot; *attuned* &middot; *attuned*
**Third person:** *attunes* &middot; **-ing form:** *attuning*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Almost always used with **to**: *attuned to the market*. American English says uh-TOON, not "uh-TYOON".

To make someone or something sensitive and well adjusted to something.

1. Years in the classroom *attune* a teacher to small changes in mood.
2. The training *attuned* the staff to the needs of older customers.
3. She is well *attuned* to what her readers want.

---

### augment

**Pronunciation:** /ɔːɡˈmɛnt/ &middot; *awg-MENT*
**Русский:** увеличивать, увеличить, дополнять
**Forms:** *augment* &middot; *augmented* &middot; *augmented*
**Third person:** *augments* &middot; **-ing form:** *augmenting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To make something larger or stronger by adding to it.

1. Many teachers *augment* their income with summer work.
2. The city *augmented* the police budget after the vote.
3. The library has *augmented* its collection with three thousand new titles.

---

### augur

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɔːɡər/ &middot; *AW-gur*
**Русский:** предвещать, сулить
**Forms:** *augur* &middot; *augured* &middot; *augured*
**Third person:** *augurs* &middot; **-ing form:** *auguring*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (the result *augurs* a difficult year), intransitive (this *augurs* well for the talks)
**Contrast:** Do not confuse *augur*, to foretell, with *auger*, a tool for drilling holes; both are said AW-gur.

To be a sign of how something is going to turn out.

1. Weak sales *augur* badly for the holiday season.
2. The early numbers *augured* well for the new director.
3. Nothing in that first meeting *augured* the trouble that followed.

---

### author

**Pronunciation:** /ˈɔːθər/ &middot; *AW-thur*
**Русский:** написать, быть автором
**Forms:** *author* &middot; *authored* &middot; *authored*
**Third person:** *authors* &middot; **-ing form:** *authoring*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To write a book, a report, or a piece of legislation.

1. Two economists *author* the annual report together.
2. She *authored* the bill that became law last spring.
3. He has *authored* more than a dozen novels.

---

### autocorrect

**Pronunciation:** /ˌɔːtoʊkəˈrɛkt/ &middot; *aw-toh-kuh-REKT*
**Русский:** автоматически исправлять (набранный текст)
**Forms:** *autocorrect* &middot; *autocorrected* &middot; *autocorrected*
**Third person:** *autocorrects* &middot; **-ing form:** *autocorrecting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (the phone *autocorrected* my message), intransitive (the field *autocorrects* as you type)

To change a typed word automatically to the one the software thinks you meant.

1. My phone always *autocorrects* his name to something else.
2. The app *autocorrected* "definitely" to "defiantly".
3. I sent the text without noticing that it had *autocorrected* the address.

---

### await

**Pronunciation:** /əˈweɪt/ &middot; *uh-WAYT*
**Русский:** ожидать, ждать
**Forms:** *await* &middot; *awaited* &middot; *awaited*
**Third person:** *awaits* &middot; **-ing form:** *awaiting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** *await* takes a direct object with no preposition: *await a decision*, but *wait **for** a decision*.

To wait for something that is expected.

1. Three more patients *await* their test results.
2. We *awaited* the verdict in complete silence.
3. He is *awaiting* trial in a county jail.

---

### awaken

**Pronunciation:** /əˈweɪkən/ &middot; *uh-WAY-kuhn*
**Русский:** будить, разбудить; пробуждаться
**Forms:** *awaken* &middot; *awakened* &middot; *awakened*
**Third person:** *awakens* &middot; **-ing form:** *awakening*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (the noise *awakened* the baby), intransitive (she *awakened* at dawn)
**Contrast:** *awaken* is regular: *awakened, awakened*. It is *awake* that is irregular: *awoke, awoken*.

To wake up, or to wake someone else up, used of feelings as well as of sleep.

1. One phone call can *awaken* the whole house.
2. The smell of coffee *awakened* something like hope in him.
3. She had *awakened* long before the alarm went off.

---

### awe

**Pronunciation:** /ɔː/ &middot; *AW*
**Русский:** внушать благоговение, поражать
**Forms:** *awe* &middot; *awed* &middot; *awed*
**Third person:** *awes* &middot; **-ing form:** *awing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The silent *e* drops before *-ing*: *awe → awing*.

To fill someone with a feeling of wonder and deep respect.

1. The scale of the canyon *awes* almost every visitor.
2. We were *awed* by the silence inside the cathedral.
3. Her performance has *awed* critics across the country.

---

### axe

**Pronunciation:** /æks/ &middot; *AKS*
**Русский:** сокращать, урезать, увольнять
**Forms:** *axe* &middot; *axed* &middot; *axed*
**Third person:** *axes* &middot; **-ing form:** *axing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American English also spells it *ax*; both spellings give *axed* and *axing*.

To get rid of something suddenly, such as a job, a show, or a budget line.

1. The network may *axe* the show after one season.
2. The company *axed* four hundred jobs in a single morning.
3. Three of the projects have already been *axed*.

---

### babble

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæbəl/ &middot; *BAB-uhl*
**Русский:** лепетать, болтать; журчать
**Forms:** *babble* &middot; *babbled* &middot; *babbled*
**Third person:** *babbles* &middot; **-ing form:** *babbling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive

To talk quickly and foolishly, or to make a soft running sound like a stream.

1. The twins *babble* to each other for hours.
2. He *babbled* something about missing his flight.
3. A little stream was *babbling* just beyond the trees.

---

### baby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbeɪbi/ &middot; *BAY-bee*
**Русский:** нянчиться, баловать, обращаться как с ребёнком
**Forms:** *baby* &middot; *babied* &middot; *babied*
**Third person:** *babies* &middot; **-ing form:** *babying*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The *-y* becomes *-ie-*: *babies, babied*, but the *-ing* form keeps it: *babying*.

To treat someone with far too much care and protection.

1. Don't *baby* him — he can carry his own bag.
2. His mother *babied* him well into his twenties.
3. You are *babying* that car; it will be fine on gravel.

---

### backdate

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbækˈdeɪt/ &middot; *bak-DAYT*
**Русский:** датировать задним числом
**Forms:** *backdate* &middot; *backdated* &middot; *backdated*
**Third person:** *backdates* &middot; **-ing form:** *backdating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To put an earlier date on a document or a payment than the real one.

1. The contract lets the company *backdate* the raise to January.
2. Someone had *backdated* the invoices by six months.
3. Executives were accused of *backdating* their stock options.

---

### backfire

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbækfaɪər/ &middot; *BAK-fy-ur*
**Русский:** давать обратный эффект, обернуться против
**Forms:** *backfire* &middot; *backfired* &middot; *backfired*
**Third person:** *backfires* &middot; **-ing form:** *backfiring*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive

To produce the opposite of the result that was intended.

1. Threats like that usually *backfire*.
2. The ad campaign *backfired* badly.
3. Their attempt to bury the story has *backfired* on them.

---

### backtrack

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæktræk/ &middot; *BAK-trak*
**Русский:** идти на попятную, отступать от сказанного; возвращаться тем же путём
**Forms:** *backtrack* &middot; *backtracked* &middot; *backtracked*
**Third person:** *backtracks* &middot; **-ing form:** *backtracking*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive

To go back the way you came, or to take back something you said earlier.

1. Let me *backtrack* a little and explain that first step again.
2. The governor *backtracked* within hours of the interview.
3. He has been *backtracking* on that promise all week.

---

### bait

**Pronunciation:** /beɪt/ &middot; *BAYT*
**Русский:** насаживать приманку; дразнить, провоцировать
**Forms:** *bait* &middot; *baited* &middot; *baited*
**Third person:** *baits* &middot; **-ing form:** *baiting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Do not confuse *bait* with *bate*, which survives only in the phrase *with bated breath*.

To put food on a hook or in a trap, or to provoke someone on purpose.

1. He *baits* the hook with a piece of shrimp.
2. She *baited* her brother until he lost his temper.
3. The traps had been *baited* the night before.

---

### balk

**Pronunciation:** /bɔːk/ &middot; *BAWK*
**Русский:** упираться, отказываться, не решаться
**Forms:** *balk* &middot; *balked* &middot; *balked*
**Third person:** *balks* &middot; **-ing form:** *balking*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** *balk* takes **at**: *balk at the cost*. British English spells it *baulk*, and the *l* is silent either way: BAWK.

To refuse suddenly to do or accept something.

1. Buyers *balk* at prices like that.
2. The board *balked* when it saw the final figure.
3. He is *balking* at signing anything before his lawyer reads it.

---

### balloon

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈluːn/ &middot; *buh-LOON*
**Русский:** резко расти, раздуваться
**Forms:** *balloon* &middot; *ballooned* &middot; *ballooned*
**Third person:** *balloons* &middot; **-ing form:** *ballooning*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive

To grow very quickly to a much larger size.

1. Repair costs can *balloon* once the walls are opened up.
2. The deficit *ballooned* during the crisis.
3. Class sizes have *ballooned* since the two schools merged.

---

### bamboozle

**Pronunciation:** /bæmˈbuːzəl/ &middot; *bam-BOO-zuhl*
**Русский:** обманывать, дурачить, морочить голову
**Forms:** *bamboozle* &middot; *bamboozled* &middot; *bamboozled*
**Third person:** *bamboozles* &middot; **-ing form:** *bamboozling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To trick or thoroughly confuse someone, usually in order to get something.

1. Salesmen like that *bamboozle* people every day.
2. He *bamboozled* his neighbors into lending him money.
3. We were completely *bamboozled* by the paperwork.

---

### bang

**Pronunciation:** /bæŋ/ &middot; *BANG*
**Русский:** стучать, ударять, хлопать (дверью)
**Forms:** *bang* &middot; *banged* &middot; *banged*
**Third person:** *bangs* &middot; **-ing form:** *banging*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (he *banged* the door), intransitive (someone was *banging* on the wall)

To hit something hard, making a loud noise.

1. Please don't *bang* the door when you leave.
2. She *banged* her knee on the table leg.
3. Someone has been *banging* on the pipes all morning.

---

### banish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbænɪʃ/ &middot; *BAN-ish*
**Русский:** изгонять, высылать; гнать прочь (мысли)
**Forms:** *banish* &middot; *banished* &middot; *banished*
**Third person:** *banishes* &middot; **-ing form:** *banishing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Ends in *-sh*, so the third person adds *-es*: *banishes*.

To send someone away from a place as a punishment, or to drive a thought out of your mind.

1. Old kings could *banish* anyone who displeased them.
2. He was *banished* from the country for twenty years.
3. She spent the whole flight *banishing* thoughts of the meeting.

---

### bank

**Pronunciation:** /bæŋk/ &middot; *BANK*
**Русский:** класть в банк, держать деньги в банке
**Forms:** *bank* &middot; *banked* &middot; *banked*
**Third person:** *banks* &middot; **-ing form:** *banking*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (she *banked* the check), intransitive (we *bank* at a credit union)

To put or keep money in a bank.

1. Where do you *bank*?
2. He *banked* most of the prize money.
3. They have *banked* with the same branch for thirty years.

---

### bankrupt

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæŋkrʌpt/ &middot; *BANK-rupt*
**Русский:** разорять, доводить до банкротства
**Forms:** *bankrupt* &middot; *bankrupted* &middot; *bankrupted*
**Third person:** *bankrupts* &middot; **-ing form:** *bankrupting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To leave a person or company with no money at all.

1. One bad season can *bankrupt* a small farm.
2. The lawsuit nearly *bankrupted* the family.
3. Medical bills have *bankrupted* thousands of households.

---

### baptize

**Pronunciation:** /bæpˈtaɪz/ &middot; *bap-TYZ*
**Русский:** крестить, окрестить
**Forms:** *baptize* &middot; *baptized* &middot; *baptized*
**Third person:** *baptizes* &middot; **-ing form:** *baptizing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American English spells it *baptize, baptized*; British English usually writes *baptise, baptised*.

To admit someone into the Christian church in a ceremony using water.

1. The priest will *baptize* the child on Sunday.
2. She was *baptized* in the same church as her mother.
3. He has *baptized* three generations of that family.

---

### bare

**Pronunciation:** /bɛr/ &middot; *BAIR*
**Русский:** обнажать, оголять, раскрывать
**Forms:** *bare* &middot; *bared* &middot; *bared*
**Third person:** *bares* &middot; **-ing form:** *baring*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** *bare* sounds exactly like *bear*, the animal and the irregular verb *bear, bore, borne*. The silent *e* drops before *-ing*: *baring*.

To uncover something, or to reveal something private.

1. The dog *bares* its teeth at strangers.
2. He *bared* his arm so the nurse could find a vein.
3. She has never *bared* that part of her life to anyone.

---

### barricade

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛrəkeɪd/ &middot; *BER-uh-kayd*
**Русский:** баррикадировать, перегораживать
**Forms:** *barricade* &middot; *barricaded* &middot; *barricaded*
**Third person:** *barricades* &middot; **-ing form:** *barricading*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To block a road or a doorway with something heavy so that nobody can get through.

1. Protesters *barricade* the main road every spring.
2. The gunman *barricaded* himself in the back office.
3. Both entrances had been *barricaded* with office furniture.

---

### bartend

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːrtɛnd/ &middot; *BAHR-tend*
**Русский:** работать барменом
**Forms:** *bartend* &middot; *bartended* &middot; *bartended*
**Third person:** *bartends* &middot; **-ing form:** *bartending*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive

To work as a bartender, serving drinks in a bar.

1. She *bartends* three nights a week to pay for school.
2. He *bartended* at a hotel downtown all summer.
3. I have been *bartending* since I was twenty-one.

---

### base

**Pronunciation:** /beɪs/ &middot; *BAYS*
**Русский:** основывать (на чём-л.), базировать
**Forms:** *base* &middot; *based* &middot; *based*
**Third person:** *bases* &middot; **-ing form:** *basing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** *base* takes **on**: *based on the evidence*. It sounds like *bass*, the low voice.

To build something on a particular fact, idea, or place as its foundation.

1. Scientists *base* that conclusion on thirty years of data.
2. The movie was *based* on a true story.
3. The company is *basing* its new office in Denver.

---

### bash

**Pronunciation:** /bæʃ/ &middot; *BASH*
**Русский:** сильно ударять; резко критиковать
**Forms:** *bash* &middot; *bashed* &middot; *bashed*
**Third person:** *bashes* &middot; **-ing form:** *bashing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Ends in *-sh*, so the third person adds *-es*: *bashes*.

To hit something hard, or to attack someone harshly in words.

1. Politicians *bash* each other for months before an election.
2. He *bashed* his elbow against the door frame.
3. The paper has been *bashing* the mayor all year.

---

### battle

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbætəl/ &middot; *BAT-uhl*
**Русский:** бороться, сражаться
**Forms:** *battle* &middot; *battled* &middot; *battled*
**Third person:** *battles* &middot; **-ing form:** *battling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (she *battled* cancer for years), intransitive (the two sides *battled* over the bill)

To fight or struggle hard against someone or something.

1. Crews *battle* wildfires along the coast every August.
2. He *battled* the insurance company for eighteen months.
3. She has been *battling* a cold all week.

---

### bawl

**Pronunciation:** /bɔːl/ &middot; *BAWL*
**Русский:** реветь, громко плакать; орать
**Forms:** *bawl* &middot; *bawled* &middot; *bawled*
**Third person:** *bawls* &middot; **-ing form:** *bawling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** *bawl* sounds exactly like *ball*.

To cry or shout very loudly.

1. Babies *bawl* when they are hungry.
2. She *bawled* through the whole ceremony.
3. He was *bawling* into the phone by the time I got there.

---

### beach

**Pronunciation:** /biːtʃ/ &middot; *BEECH*
**Русский:** вытаскивать на берег; выбрасываться на берег
**Forms:** *beach* &middot; *beached* &middot; *beached*
**Third person:** *beaches* &middot; **-ing form:** *beaching*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (*beach* the canoe above the tide line), intransitive (three whales *beached* overnight)
**Contrast:** Ends in *-ch*, so the third person adds *-es*: *beaches*. It sounds like *beech*, the tree.

To pull a boat up onto the shore, or of a sea animal, to become stranded on land.

1. We usually *beach* the kayaks near the point.
2. A young whale *beached* south of the pier on Tuesday.
3. The ferry has been *beached* since the storm.

---

### beam

**Pronunciation:** /biːm/ &middot; *BEEM*
**Русский:** сиять, лучезарно улыбаться; передавать (сигнал)
**Forms:** *beam* &middot; *beamed* &middot; *beamed*
**Third person:** *beams* &middot; **-ing form:** *beaming*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (they *beamed* the signal to the station), intransitive (she *beamed* at the news)

To smile broadly with happiness, or to send out light or a signal.

1. The satellites *beam* the game to half the world.
2. She *beamed* when they called her name.
3. He was *beaming* all the way through the ceremony.

---

### beautify

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbjuːtəfaɪ/ &middot; *BYOO-tuh-fy*
**Русский:** украшать, украсить, приукрашивать
**Forms:** *beautify* &middot; *beautified* &middot; *beautified*
**Third person:** *beautifies* &middot; **-ing form:** *beautifying*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The *-y* becomes *-ie-*: *beautifies, beautified*, but the *-ing* form keeps it: *beautifying*.

To make a place or a thing look more attractive.

1. Volunteers *beautify* the park every spring.
2. The city *beautified* the waterfront ahead of the festival.
3. They have been *beautifying* the old train station for months.

---

### befit

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈfɪt/ &middot; *bi-FIT*
**Русский:** подобать, приличествовать; соответствовать
**Forms:** *befit* &middot; *befitted* &middot; *befitted*
**Third person:** *befits* &middot; **-ing form:** *befitting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The final t doubles: befit - befitted - befitting.

To be suitable or proper for someone or something.

1. Wear something that will *befit* a formal dinner.
2. The town gave the old teacher a send-off that *befitted* forty years of service.
3. She accepted the prize with a modesty *befitting* her age.

---

### befuddle

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈfʌdəl/ &middot; *bi-FUD-uhl*
**Русский:** сбивать с толку, запутать
**Forms:** *befuddle* &middot; *befuddled* &middot; *befuddled*
**Third person:** *befuddles* &middot; **-ing form:** *befuddling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To confuse someone so badly that they cannot think clearly.

1. Long legal sentences *befuddle* most readers.
2. The magician *befuddled* the audience with one simple card.
3. He looked completely *befuddled* when I mentioned the deadline.

---

### begrudge

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈɡrʌdʒ/ &middot; *bi-GRUJ*
**Русский:** завидовать; жалеть (что-л. для кого-л.)
**Forms:** *begrudge* &middot; *begrudged* &middot; *begrudged*
**Third person:** *begrudges* &middot; **-ing form:** *begrudging*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Takes two objects: begrudge SOMEONE SOMETHING (begrudge him his success).

To feel resentment about what someone has or about giving something up.

1. I don't *begrudge* her the promotion; she earned it.
2. Nobody *begrudged* him a day off after that week.
3. She has never *begrudged* a dollar spent on her children's schooling.

---

### beguile

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈɡaɪl/ &middot; *bi-GYL*
**Русский:** очаровывать, обольщать; обманывать
**Forms:** *beguile* &middot; *beguiled* &middot; *beguiled*
**Third person:** *beguiles* &middot; **-ing form:** *beguiling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To charm someone, often in a way that misleads them.

1. Street performers *beguile* the crowd with impossible tricks.
2. The salesman *beguiled* them into signing a terrible lease.
3. It is a *beguiling* idea, but the numbers do not work.

---

### behead

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈhɛd/ &middot; *bi-HED*
**Русский:** обезглавливать, обезглавить
**Forms:** *behead* &middot; *beheaded* &middot; *beheaded*
**Third person:** *beheads* &middot; **-ing form:** *beheading*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To cut off someone's head.

1. In the play the queen threatens to *behead* anyone who lies to her.
2. Anne Boleyn was *beheaded* in 1536.
3. Two of the marble figures had been *beheaded* long before the museum bought them.

---

### behoove

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈhuːv/ &middot; *bi-HOOV*
**Русский:** надлежать, следовать (кому-л. сделать что-л.)
**Forms:** *behoove* &middot; *behooved* &middot; *behooved*
**Third person:** *behooves* &middot; **-ing form:** *behooving*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American behoove, British behove. Almost always used with it: it behooves someone TO do something.

To be necessary or sensible for someone to do something.

1. It would *behoove* you to read the contract before you sign it.
2. It *behooved* the company to settle the case quickly.
3. It *behooves* every driver to check the tires before a long trip.

---

### bejewel

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈdʒuːəl/ &middot; *bi-JOO-uhl*
**Русский:** украшать драгоценностями
**Forms:** *bejewel* &middot; *bejeweled* &middot; *bejeweled*
**Third person:** *bejewels* &middot; **-ing form:** *bejeweling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American keeps one l: bejeweled, bejeweling. British doubles it: bejewelled, bejewelling.

To decorate something with jewels.

1. The designers *bejewel* every gown by hand.
2. She *bejeweled* the costume with hundreds of tiny stones.
3. A *bejeweled* crown sat alone in the glass case.

---

### belabor

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈleɪbər/ &middot; *bi-LAY-ber*
**Русский:** мусолить (тему), раздувать; разглагольствовать
**Forms:** *belabor* &middot; *belabored* &middot; *belabored*
**Third person:** *belabors* &middot; **-ing form:** *belaboring*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American belabor, British belabour.

To keep going over something long after the point has been made.

1. I don't want to *belabor* the point, but we are out of time.
2. The speaker *belabored* the same statistic for ten minutes.
3. He has a habit of *belaboring* details everyone already understands.

---

### belch

**Pronunciation:** /bɛltʃ/ &middot; *BELCH*
**Русский:** рыгать, рыгнуть; извергать (дым, пламя)
**Forms:** *belch* &middot; *belched* &middot; *belched*
**Third person:** *belches* &middot; **-ing form:** *belching*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (the engine belched smoke), intransitive (he belched loudly)

To let gas out of the stomach noisily through the mouth, or to send something out in thick clouds.

1. Please don't *belch* at the table.
2. The old bus *belched* black smoke as it pulled away.
3. Factory chimneys were *belching* steam into the cold morning air.

---

### beleaguer

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈliːɡər/ &middot; *bi-LEE-ger*
**Русский:** осаждать; изводить, донимать
**Forms:** *beleaguer* &middot; *beleaguered* &middot; *beleaguered*
**Third person:** *beleaguers* &middot; **-ing form:** *beleaguering*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To surround someone with troubles or with an army so that there is no easy escape.

1. Lawsuits *beleaguer* the company on every side.
2. Bad weather *beleaguered* the expedition for a week.
3. The *beleaguered* mayor resigned on Friday.

---

### belie

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈlaɪ/ &middot; *bi-LY*
**Русский:** противоречить, опровергать; скрывать истинное положение
**Forms:** *belie* &middot; *belied* &middot; *belied*
**Third person:** *belies* &middot; **-ing form:** *belying*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Drop the -e and keep the y: belies, belied, belying. Not the same as lie (to lie down, to tell a lie).

To give a false impression of something, or to show that something is not true.

1. A cheerful tone can *belie* real anxiety.
2. The calm surface *belied* the current running underneath.
3. His boyish face has always *belied* his age.

---

### bellow

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛloʊ/ &middot; *BEL-oh*
**Русский:** реветь, орать; проорать
**Forms:** *bellow* &middot; *bellowed* &middot; *bellowed*
**Third person:** *bellows* &middot; **-ing form:** *bellowing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (he bellowed an order), intransitive (she bellowed at the referee)
**Contrast:** Not the word below: bellow is BEL-oh with stress on the first syllable, below is bi-LOH.

To shout or roar in a deep, very loud voice.

1. Coaches *bellow* instructions from the sideline all game.
2. The sergeant *bellowed* an order across the yard.
3. We could hear him *bellowing* at the umpire from the top row.

---

### bellyache

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛliˌeɪk/ &middot; *BEL-ee-ayk*
**Русский:** ныть, жаловаться
**Forms:** *bellyache* &middot; *bellyached* &middot; *bellyached*
**Third person:** *bellyaches* &middot; **-ing form:** *bellyaching*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** Informal. Complain bellyache ABOUT something. The -y stays because belly joins ache as one word.

To complain on and on in a whiny, annoying way.

1. They *bellyache* about the coffee every single morning.
2. He *bellyached* for an hour about the new parking rules.
3. Stop *bellyaching* and finish the job.

---

### belt

**Pronunciation:** /bɛlt/ &middot; *BELT*
**Русский:** сильно ударить, врезать; запустить (мяч)
**Forms:** *belt* &middot; *belted* &middot; *belted*
**Third person:** *belts* &middot; **-ing form:** *belting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To hit something or someone hard with a swinging blow.

1. Don't *belt* your brother over a video game.
2. She *belted* the ball over the left-field fence.
3. He has *belted* thirty home runs this season.

---

### bemoan

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈmoʊn/ &middot; *bi-MOHN*
**Русский:** сетовать, сокрушаться; оплакивать
**Forms:** *bemoan* &middot; *bemoaned* &middot; *bemoaned*
**Third person:** *bemoans* &middot; **-ing form:** *bemoaning*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To complain about something or express sorrow over it.

1. Critics *bemoan* the loss of small bookstores.
2. She *bemoaned* the state of the roads the whole drive home.
3. Owners are *bemoaning* another rise in rent.

---

### bemuse

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈmjuːz/ &middot; *bi-MYOOZ*
**Русский:** озадачивать, приводить в замешательство
**Forms:** *bemuse* &middot; *bemused* &middot; *bemused*
**Third person:** *bemuses* &middot; **-ing form:** *bemusing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Not amuse. Bemused means puzzled, not entertained.

To puzzle or bewilder someone.

1. The question seemed to *bemuse* the witness.
2. His answer *bemused* the entire committee.
3. She watched the argument with a *bemused* smile.

---

### benchmark

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛntʃmɑːrk/ &middot; *BENCH-mark*
**Русский:** сравнивать с эталоном, тестировать (производительность)
**Forms:** *benchmark* &middot; *benchmarked* &middot; *benchmarked*
**Third person:** *benchmarks* &middot; **-ing form:** *benchmarking*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Benchmark something AGAINST something else.

To measure something against a standard or against what rivals achieve.

1. We *benchmark* our prices against the two largest chains.
2. The lab *benchmarked* the new chip at twice the old speed.
3. They are *benchmarking* salaries across the whole industry.

---

### besmirch

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈsmɜːrtʃ/ &middot; *bi-SMURCH*
**Русский:** пятнать, порочить (репутацию)
**Forms:** *besmirch* &middot; *besmirched* &middot; *besmirched*
**Third person:** *besmirches* &middot; **-ing form:** *besmirching*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To damage someone's reputation or good name.

1. He said the rumors were meant to *besmirch* his record.
2. One scandal *besmirched* a family name that had stood for a century.
3. She sued the paper for *besmirching* her reputation.

---

### best

**Pronunciation:** /bɛst/ &middot; *BEST*
**Русский:** побеждать, одолеть, превзойти
**Forms:** *best* &middot; *bested* &middot; *bested*
**Third person:** *bests* &middot; **-ing form:** *besting*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** As a verb, best means to beat someone, not to do your best.

To defeat someone in a contest or an argument.

1. Very few players can *best* her at chess.
2. The underdog *bested* the champion in three straight sets.
3. He has been *bested* only twice all season.

---

### better

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛtər/ &middot; *BET-er*
**Русский:** улучшать, улучшить; превзойти
**Forms:** *better* &middot; *bettered* &middot; *bettered*
**Third person:** *betters* &middot; **-ing form:** *bettering*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** The stress is on the first syllable, so the t does not double again: bettered, bettering.

To improve something, or to do better than an earlier result.

1. He took night classes to *better* his chances of promotion.
2. She *bettered* the school record by two full seconds.
3. The program helps families who are *bettering* their standard of living.

---

### bevel

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛvəl/ &middot; *BEV-uhl*
**Русский:** скашивать (край), снимать фаску
**Forms:** *bevel* &middot; *beveled* &middot; *beveled*
**Third person:** *bevels* &middot; **-ing form:** *beveling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American keeps one l: beveled, beveling. British doubles it: bevelled, bevelling.

To cut a sloping edge on a piece of wood, glass, or metal.

1. We *bevel* the edges so that nobody cuts a finger.
2. The carpenter *beveled* the edge of the mirror.
3. A *beveled* glass panel catches the light beautifully.

---

### bewitch

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈwɪtʃ/ &middot; *bi-WICH*
**Русский:** околдовывать, заколдовать; очаровывать
**Forms:** *bewitch* &middot; *bewitched* &middot; *bewitched*
**Third person:** *bewitches* &middot; **-ing form:** *bewitching*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive

To put a spell on someone, or to charm someone completely.

1. The old stories say that a single glance can *bewitch* a traveler.
2. Her voice *bewitched* the whole audience.
3. He stood at the window, *bewitched* by the view.

---

### bias

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaɪəs/ &middot; *BY-uhs*
**Русский:** склонять, предвзято настраивать
**Forms:** *bias* &middot; *biased* &middot; *biased*
**Third person:** *biases* &middot; **-ing form:** *biasing*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** American spelling keeps one s: biased, biasing. Bias someone AGAINST or TOWARD something.

To influence someone or something unfairly in one direction.

1. A loaded question can *bias* an entire survey.
2. Early news reports *biased* the jury against him.
3. The sample is *biased* toward younger users.

---

### bicycle

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaɪsɪkəl/ &middot; *BY-si-kuhl*
**Русский:** ездить на велосипеде
**Forms:** *bicycle* &middot; *bicycled* &middot; *bicycled*
**Third person:** *bicycles* &middot; **-ing form:** *bicycling*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** intransitive
**Contrast:** The final -e drops before -ing: bicycling, not bicycleing.

To travel somewhere by bicycle.

1. They *bicycle* to work in almost any weather.
2. We *bicycled* along the coast for three days.
3. She spent the summer *bicycling* through Vermont.

---

### bifurcate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaɪfərkeɪt/ &middot; *BY-fer-kayt*
**Русский:** раздваиваться, разветвляться; разделять надвое
**Forms:** *bifurcate* &middot; *bifurcated* &middot; *bifurcated*
**Third person:** *bifurcates* &middot; **-ing form:** *bifurcating*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** both — transitive (the judge bifurcated the trial), intransitive (the trail bifurcates)

To divide into two branches or parts.

1. Streams like this often *bifurcate* before they reach the sea.
2. The judge *bifurcated* the trial into a guilt phase and a damages phase.
3. The company has *bifurcated* into two separate businesses.

---

### bilk

**Pronunciation:** /bɪlk/ &middot; *BILK*
**Русский:** обманывать, надувать (на деньги)
**Forms:** *bilk* &middot; *bilked* &middot; *bilked*
**Third person:** *bilks* &middot; **-ing form:** *bilking*
**Verb class:** regular
**Transitivity:** transitive
**Contrast:** Usually bilk someone OUT OF money.

To cheat someone out of money.

1. Scammers *bilk* older people out of their savings every year.
2. He *bilked* investors out of two million dollars.
3. She was charged with *bilking* the charity she ran.

---

### binge

**Pronunciation:** /bɪndʒ/ &middot; *BINJ*
**Русский:** предаваться излишествам; смотреть или есть запоем
**Forms:** *binge* &middot; *binged* &middot; *binged*
**Third person:** *binges* &middot; **-ing form:** *bingeing*
**Verb class:** regular
